People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Hi, doctor. It's really nice to meet you too. Thank you so much for fitting me in. 

Um... I guess I'm here because... well, I've been doing some kind of weird things at work lately. Nothing bad! I mean, I'm a bookkeeping clerk, so I'm usually very on top of things, but... I've just started doing some little things that don't exactly make sense? 

Like, um... I'll be sorting things and I'll start lining up receipts all across the floor instead of filing them. Or I'll catch myself tapping the calculator, you know, just tapping it in sets of nine. And... this is a little embarrassing to admit, but sometimes I'll just stand by the copier with my hands held up in the air, just kind of frozen there until someone comes by and asks if I'm okay. 

I know it probably sounds a little strange, but I promise it's not a huge deal or anything. I just wanted to make sure I'm doing the right thing by coming in to get it checked out. Is that... does that sound like the kind of thing I should be here for?

And... this is a little embarrassing to admit, but sometimes I'll just stand by the copier with my hands held up in the air, just kind of frozen there until someone comes by and asks if I'm okay. \n\nI know it probably sounds a little strange, but I promise it's not a huge deal or anything. I just wanted to make sure I'm doing the right thing by coming in to get it checked out. Is that... does that sound like the kind of thing I should be here for?\n\nDoctor: When you tap the calculator in sets of nine, does it feel like something you have to do to relieve a strong anxious thought or urge?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, not really. It's not like an anxious thing or an urge I have to fight off, I don't think. It's just... I just sort of find myself doing it. \n\nLike, I'll just be sitting there, and it just happens. It doesn't feel bad or scary, just a little silly, really. I hope that's okay? I mean, I know it sounds a little odd, but I'm not, like, obsessing over it or anything like that. I just thought I should mention it because I want to give you all the right information. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have there been times lately when your mood felt unusually high or full of energy?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess maybe a little? But usually in a good way, I think! There are some weeks where I just feel... really, really sharp. Like, almost unstoppable, honestly. I'll get this burst of energy and feel like I can just map out my entire future in a single night. It's actually kind of nice to feel that motivated, you know? \n\nBut, um... I guess I should admit, there's a part of it I'm not super proud of. Sometimes when I'm feeling like that, if my boss or, like, a classmate gives me a small correction on something... I can get a little defensive. I might snap at them a bit harder than I mean to. \n\nI always feel terrible about it afterward, because I really try to be easy to work with! I promise I'm not a mean person, it just sort of bubbles over before I can catch it. Is it... does that sound like something I should be worried about? I hope it doesn't make me sound awful.\n\nDoctor: When you have those sharp, unstoppable periods, do you need much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, actually... I guess I do sleep a lot less during those times. But it doesn't feel like a bad thing! \n\nThere have been a few times, like, I'll go for several nights only sleeping maybe two or three hours. But I still feel completely ready to go to work and to my classes. It's almost like... like sleep is just getting in the way, taking time away from getting my whole life figured out, you know? \n\nI know two or three hours doesn't sound like a lot, but I promise I'm not, like, falling asleep at my desk or anything. I'm still doing a good job! I just have so much energy that I don't really need the rest. Is that... is it okay that I don't need as much sleep sometimes? I hope that's not a huge problem.\n\nDoctor: Do the times when you freeze or line things up happen only when you're in those high-energy, low-sleep periods?\n\nPatient: Um, no, actually. It's not just when I'm feeling all energized like that. \n\nLike, there was this one stretch... it was maybe a few weeks, I think? I was sleeping pretty normally, like a full night, and I didn't have that super sharp, unstoppable feeling at all. I felt pretty regular. But... um... I still felt really pulled into those number patterns. And... well, I still heard the voice, too. \n\nI hope that doesn't make things too complicated to figure out? I know it's probably easier if it all happens at the same time, but I really want to make sure I'm giving you the whole picture. I'm trying to be as helpful as I can, I promise. And he just says really normal things, like... like, \"check line seven,\" or \"you missed the sign.\" \n\nIt's... it's so clear that I usually turn around to see who's talking to me, even though I know I'm by myself. I just... I didn't want to make a big fuss, you know? Since it wasn't keeping me from doing my bookkeeping or anything, I thought maybe it was just a quirk I could handle on my own without bothering anyone. \n\nI hope you're not frustrated with me for waiting so long to tell someone. I really am trying to do the right thing now. Um... yeah, I have. I hope that's the right answer, I just want to be honest with you. 

There was actually a stretch, um, maybe a few weeks or so, where I wasn't feeling all revved up like that. I was actually sleeping pretty normally, getting my eight hours, and I didn't have that unstoppable energy. I was just kind of... normal, I guess?

I promise I'm not a mean person, it just sort of bubbles over before I can catch it. Is it... does that sound like something I should be worried about? I hope it doesn't make me sound awful.\n\nDoctor: When you have those sharp, unstoppable periods, do you need much less sleep than usual?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah, actually...
